## 2. What remains, in this order

### 2.1 Debt 7l: an extension error that no message can be made of crashes the daemon

**Status.** Started on `dd29a03`. Nothing is committed or pushed. The local measurement was stopped before it produced evidence, so start from the current `main` and measure first.

**Defect.** Found by debt 7k's full review (R7k-7, Medium); it predates patch `0050`.
- Extension code can throw a value that no message can be made of: `Object.create(null)`, a Proxy whose `getPrototypeOf` trap throws, or an Error whose `message` getter throws.
- In the prepared series:
  - `daemon/core/src/rpc/errors.ts:56`, `toRpcError`, formats a rejection with `e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)`. A handler rejected with such a value throws inside `processLine`, and autoskd crashes. This was reproduced on the base and on the 7k candidate: an `onTransit` function that throws `Object.create(null)` at `task.enroll` gives `TypeError: No default value at toRpcError … at processLine`, and the daemon is gone.
  - `daemon/core/src/extensions/loader.ts:297` has its own `errMsg` with the same expression. It is used for `failed to import` (`:428`) and `factory threw` (`:447`). A factory that throws such a value itself makes the loader's catch throw, and the project fails to open.
  - `daemon/core/src/extensions/servedDistributions.ts:64` carries a third copy.
- Patch `0050` already made `errMsg` in `daemon/core/src/engine/types.ts` and in `registry.ts` total, with the placeholder `an unprintable value`.

**Carried from debt 7k's narrow re-review (Low).** Fix these in the same patch `0051`:
- **R7k-8.** `0050`'s total `errMsg` maps a printable non-string message to the placeholder in every engine catch, `finalizeFailed` included; examples are an Error whose `message` is `404`, `undefined`, `null` or `{a:1}`. Keep `String(m)` inside the try, and use the placeholder only when the conversion throws.
- **R7k-9.** `registry.ts` `admitWorkflow` indents its whole body six spaces to keep this lock-anchored line at six spaces: `      if (workflow.graphDigest !== undefined) admission.document = workflow.graphDigest;`. Move the `if (identity) { … }` block into the method, so the body takes its natural indent and the anchored line stays byte-identical.
- **R7k-10**, wording:
  - (a) the `addWorkflow` and `addAgent` doc comments say any read that throws before the store skips the registration, while an optional hook whose read throws is skipped alone;
  - (b) `docs/extensions.md` calls the skipping reads "required fields and the shape", but a throwing `description` getter skips the registration too;
  - (c) a test comment in `extensions.registry.test.ts`, on the `graphDigest` case, counts three reads where there are four;
  - (d) a test comment claims the `addAgent` rollback is exercised, but it is not observable there;
  - (e) a comment says "Two outcomes are new here", which is history, not behavior;
  - (f) the docs say an unpinned task "parks at resume": a parked unpinned task is refused at resume (1004), and a working one parks at the next dispatch.

**Acceptance.**
- No value thrown by extension code crashes the daemon or fails a project open because it cannot be formatted, shown by a test and a live run.
- The operator sees an honest error.
- The RPC error format is unchanged for every value that formats today.
- R7k-8, R7k-9 and R7k-10 are closed.
- `npm test`, the validators and the series' suites are green, and the review follows §3.

**Work type:** bug-fix (§6).

## 3. How each debt is run

1. Branch from the current `main`, in a clean worktree.
2. Measure first, by running code. Whenever a form could refuse or re-identify something accepted before, measure the upgrade path too, with open pinned tasks and held distributions; this is the lesson of debt 7h. Write the decision before code, with the forms not taken and why.
3. Write the red tests first, then the fix. Then run mutants: each must die on its test file and on the full daemon suite, and each mutant's log holds only its own diff.
4. Do the five-path ripple (§4) and the full verification.
5. Get an independent review. Cross-family review is mandatory, and self-review does not close a ticket. A reviewer from the same model family does not satisfy it. If this session cannot get a review from another model family, open the PR with CI green and stop for the owner's decision; do not merge.
6. Handle findings.
   - Medium or above blocks: fix it in a fix round, then get one narrow re-review from the same reviewer.
   - A Low is fixed in a fix round that happens anyway; otherwise it is carried into the next debt that edits the same file.
   - A pre-existing defect found on the way becomes its own debt, measured first.
7. Commit only the approved tree onto the recorded base, with no hooks changing it. Then:
   - open the PR;
   - run CI: push, pull_request, and a `workflow_dispatch` of `validate-traycer-parity.yml`;
   - merge with `gh pr merge <n> --merge --match-head-commit <sha>`;
   - confirm that `main` CI is green and the mutation baseline is unchanged.

**CI lesson.** The `workflow_dispatch` run of "Validate program design registries" carries the mutation job. If that dispatch run starts before the pull_request run of the same workflow, a watcher that reads the latest run per workflow reports green too early. Before merging, confirm the dispatch run by its id: completed, success, the mutation job a success, and the baseline counts of §1.

## 4. The autosk compatibility series

- **Prepare.** Run `node scripts/prepare-autosk.mjs <new empty directory>`; its write-tree must equal the manifest's `result_tree`. The script compares real paths, so give it a path that is not behind a symlink (on macOS, `/private/tmp/…`, not `/tmp/…`). The read-only fetch it makes from `wierdbytes/autosk` is allowed.
- **A new patch** is the diff of your edits against the prepared tree. Write it to `compat/autosk/patches/00NN-<slug>.patch` and append it last to the manifest with its sha256. `result_tree` is recomputed by preparing again from an empty directory. A patch on `main` is never edited in place; the tip patch of an open PR may be rewritten.
- **The five paths** that move with a patch:
  - the patch;
  - `compat/autosk/manifest.v1.json`: the entry and `result_tree`;
  - `test/prepare-autosk.test.mjs`: the `result_tree` pin and the ordered list;
  - `docs/runtime/autosk-compatibility.md`: a row with the new tree marked "the current result_tree", with the marker taken off the previous row;
  - `resources/design-candidate/design-candidate.v1.json`: only `/files/7/sha256`, set to the manifest's sha256, and `/candidate_digest`, written as `JSON.stringify(value, null, 2) + "\n"`. `git diff --stat` shows 2 insertions and 2 deletions, and `validate:design-candidate` passes.
- **The lock.** `validate:runtime-identity-lock` keeps `requirements=25` and the lock digest, and `counted_across` equals the number of patches. Every anchored line stays byte-identical, text and indentation; a try block around an anchored line re-indents it.
- **Suites in the prepared tree.**
  - Run `cd daemon && bun install --frozen-lockfile`, then `bun test` and `bun run typecheck`. The store tests need `AUTOSK_STORE_LOCK_BIN` pointing at a helper built with `go build ./cmd/autosk-store-lock`; build it without `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod`.
  - Run `go test ./...`. A first run may show the cold-start timeouts `TestAutoInit_NonTTYCreatesProject` and `TestAutoSpawn_ReadSucceeds`; they pass alone and on a rerun.
  - Run pi-tools' `bun test` and its typecheck.
- **Suites in the repository.** Run every `npm run validate:*` (46 of them), then `npm test` last and alone. Under heavy load, `test/runtime-preflight-runner.test.mjs` and `test/measure-design-inputs.test.mjs` can miss wall-clock checks; rerun them alone.
- **Build reproducibility.** A fresh prepared tree of `3f3051a7` builds autoskd `3ae1a649ae3649d8e59be8a638dc01d0b90cb6604a04cd4baeda7382502dd430` with `bun build --compile core/src/index.ts`. Label every binary with the write-tree of the tree it came from.

## 5. Standing constraints

- No force push. Never bypass branch protection or a tool limit: on a policy block, record the exact error and stop.
- Never write to `wierdbytes/autosk`. Reading it is allowed.
- Publish no secrets and no private archives. Do not widen token scopes. Make no purchases, plan changes or paid dependencies, and no deploy to real users.
- Run autoskd, autosk or any test that spawns the daemon only with `HOME` set to a fresh directory of your own, never the real `HOME`, not even for `--version`:
  - put `AUTOSK_SOCK`, `TMPDIR` and `AUTOSK_STORE_LOCK_BIN` inside it, and set `AUTOSK_NO_AUTO_INSTALL=1`;
  - start autoskd only as `serve --tcp 127.0.0.1:0`; without `--tcp` it listens on `0.0.0.0:7077`;
  - leave no daemon running.
- Bug fixes are test-first. Add no dependency and update none. Never use `git stash`. Never fabricate a PASS. Take timestamps only from measurements.
- One active item at a time: the next one starts when the previous is merged and `main` is green.

## 6. Bug-fix method, summarized

- A bug-fix ticket starts from an established root cause with runtime evidence attached. Verify the repro once on your surface. If the cause is not established or the evidence does not reproduce, stop and report; never investigate and fix in one step.
- A bug you cannot reproduce you cannot prove fixed. Reproduce it on the matching surface.
- Write the failing test before the fix. After the fix, the original repro passes on the same surface.
- Ship the smallest change the evidence justifies; a guard that only "might help" does not ship.
- Report what was broken, the root cause with its evidence, the fix, and the failing and passing runs with their commands.

## 7. State that lives only on the owner's machine

The owner's machine holds the orchestration ledger, the evidence directory of every dispatch, and the Traycer artifacts: tickets, the debt-closing sequence and the panel records. None of it is in this repository, and this file summarizes what a cloud session needs. Ask the owner for anything missing rather than inferring it.

  - Fix, review routing: `modelOf` and `familyOf` moved into `cross-family-review.mjs`. `validate:provider-preflight` re-exports them, so the roster check, the code review and the panel roles all resolve families through one function. A participant is a route or a model id. The literal `human` excludes nothing. Anything the partition does not resolve is refused with the host-local `review_family_unknown`, which replaces `review_family_collision`. That covers a harness or vendor label, a tool name, an unlisted model, a missing partition and an empty author set. `reviewerRoute` ranks `master_order` minus the union of author and fixer families; the module has no order or alias table of its own now. `reviewAdmission` takes the available exact reviewer routes explicitly and refuses without them. Those are the routes provider preflight admitted, today the `REQUIRED_PANEL` routes. It keeps only ranked families that have such a route, and returns `reviewer_route`: the chosen family's first route in the order listed, since Muse is served both as `meta/` and as `cursor/`. It parks with `review_no_external_family` when no family is left. So with the live roster Codex gets Muse first, and the union Codex, Muse, Grok and Opus parks instead of going to Kimi, which has no route.
  - Fix, panel roles: `panelRoles` in `panel.mjs` picks Lead: the first `master_order` family outside the author/fixer union that holds a seat. The Supplementary seats are the ones inside the union. `runPanel` calls it before any seat runs and refuses with the host-local `panel_lead_not_external` when no seated family is external. A recorded Lead is kept while its family stays outside the union and refused once a fixer puts that family inside. `runPanel` returns `roles` and passes them to `computeGate`. Route availability does not move Lead: a seat without an admitted route makes the panel incomplete under the all-seats-answer rule, and 01 §3 and 03 §4 now say so.
  - Fix, Supplementary rule: `liftedByLead` in `finding-registry.mjs` sits above `computeGate` and below line 89, so the named survivor at :79 did not move. A critical or high raised only by Supplementary seats blocks until Lead rules, and only `lead_ruling {seat: roles.lead, outcome: disagreed}` lifts it. A finding that another seat also raised is not liftable. With no roles recorded, nothing lifts. Supersession drops the ruling and the roles, as it drops the contest, so the next candidate's roles are recomputed from its own author and fixer set. The registry schema has optional `roles` and `lead_ruling` (the reason is required). `validate:finding-registry` gates with the same predicate. It refuses:
    - a Lead that is also Supplementary;
    - a role outside the declared seats;
    - a ruling from a seat other than Lead;
    - a ruling with no recorded Lead;
    - a ruling on a finding a seat outside Supplementary also raised;
    - a ruling on a finding below high.

    Roles enter `registry_digest` once recorded, so the example's digest is unchanged. The contract gains a bullet in §7 and one in §10.

M3: the host sees only the copy of the authorization it is handed — signed as terminal or expired by now is `acceptance_stale`, revoked after signing is `acceptance_missing` (its bytes are no longer the signed ones), and a revocation the host never sees is `integrateApproved`'s authorization-head check (ADR-091, `epic-staging.md` §5, IA §5). L1: `issued_at` not after now and not before the request's `created_at` when it has one (`decision_identity_stale`); "not after `expires_at`" follows, since an expired request is refused first. L2: every record field is shape-checked (hex, RFC 3339, integers, NFC text without NUL or surrogates) under the caller's code. L3: the same answer under another record for an answered request is `decision_identity_stale`. L4: the authorization must say `issued_by: user_decision_record`, and its UserDecisionRecord must name the Epic and the anchor version (`anchorVersion`) the acceptance is asked at; the closed-schema check of the record is named open (`validateJsonSchema` lives in `scripts/`). L5: 02 §7 states the `domain\0` + canonical bytes convention and that `user_decision` and `policy` may both be non-null, never both null. Forms not taken: the host as second writer of staging with 02 §2 narrowed (breaks the one-writer boundary on the ref the aggregate PASS is bound to); the daemon as staging writer (a second writer with its own journal); committed signed staging wire goldens (every golden is signed by the daemon key `ref-custody-policy.example.json` binds, whose private key is not in the repository; a new key would move the policy digest and every planning body, receipt and `helper_evidence`); a `preflight_primitives` entry for the helper (by ADR-092 that list is exactly what the daemon capability preflight requires; custody is a project-open bootstrap check).

  - Decision (the recommended one, kept): `composeAuthorization` composes the record before the question (project, Epic, scope `epic:<epic-id>`, target, one transition to the commit that lands, tree and `ordered_ticket_commit_oids` from the receipts in order, anchor digest and heads from the identity, run/plan/classifier/chain/expiry from the plan); the acceptance packet presents it (`integration_authorization`) and its `accept` option carries `signed_payload_hash` = `authorizationPayloadHash(record)`, which `answerRequest` requires as the record's `payload_hash` (else `decision_approver_mismatch`; free text on such an option `decision_packet_incomplete`); `acceptanceFromDecision` completes the record with the verified decision's id/digest, runs the auto-policy's `assertAuthorized` (now also checking scope, applied commits, anchor digest and heads), writes an acceptance with `integration_authorization_id`/`_sha256` (`digest('autosk-flow/integration-authorization-record/v1', record)`), and returns the record for the daemon. `acceptanceErrors`/`casAdmission` refuse, for both kinds, a missing or malformed reference, a CAS asked without the record or under another (`acceptance_missing`), and a named record for another Epic/target/transition/tree, terminal or expired at `nowMs` (`acceptance_stale`). The identity binds `heads` (the four fields); "in advance" means before the acceptance step for that exact identity. R7-8: the IA worked example with its OIDs rewritten to 64 hex failed the IA schema in all five OID fields (seven values), and so did the record `composeAuthorization` composed for a 64-hex staging state (six values; the request schema's draft the same), while the staging schema admitted the staging example in 64 hex; a staging record with a 40-hex base and a 64-hex staging commit passed the staging schema and `validateStaging`, `composeAuthorization` composed a 40 → 64 record from that state, and `askCustody` admitted a `not_applied` answer observing a 64-hex OID for a 40-hex request. R7-31: in a SHA-256 repository `readRef` returned `null` for a present ref, `reflogDepth` 0 (2 in the SHA-1 twin), `reflogEntries` nothing, and `observeRef` `{ref: 'other', reflog: 'unknown', oid: null}` for a ref at the expected commit, which `publicationDecision` parks as `planning_ref_foreign_movement`; `listTree` in `src/host/delta-driver.mjs` (same class, not named by R7-31) read no tree entry, and `applyDelta` refused `foreign_ref_movement` before applying because `readRef` missed the staging ref. A walk of every schema under `resources/` (the new `test/object-format.test.mjs`, red on the base): a 40-only OID pattern outside a branch `object_format: sha1` selects in 14 files — the project repository's objects in the IA schema, its draft in the request schema, `artifact-write-receipt`, `run-record`, `evidence-manifest`, `project-status`, `iteration-result`, `static-analysis-result`, `verification-batch`, `feature-map`, `walkthrough`, and this repository's own commits in `anchor-pack`, the issue inventory and the matrix; 16 records with two or more OIDs had no one-format rule (ten of those, plus staging, `approved-delta`, `execution-base`, `delivery-profile`, `project-instructions-lock` — which names `object_format` without tying the widths to it — and the helper intents); eight more have their format held by their validator.
  - Decision (the recommended one, kept): one rule, defined once in `src/runtime/contracts.mjs` — `OBJECT_FORMATS` (`sha1: 40`, `sha256: 64`), `oidFormat` (the format a full OID's width names, else `null`), `oneObjectFormat` (the one format of a set, else `null`); `assertOid` and `gitObjectOid` read the same table, behavior unchanged. A record that names `object_format` ties its widths to it; one that does not, to its own OIDs, as `deliveryCompleted` does to the base. The schemas state the rule themselves: every OID field of the project repository's objects admits both widths, and every record with two or more OIDs carries an `allOf` one-format rule (`if` all 40 / `else` all 64), since JSON Schema can express "one width across fields" and a schema is what the daemon and the trusted client read. Forms not taken: an `object_format` field in the IA and staging records (a new required field in a signed record, its payload, the request schema and every example, while the OIDs already carry the format); widening the patterns only (a mixed record stays schema-valid); the rule in validators only (not seen by a reader of the schema; left as is only where the validator already holds it); asking git for the format on every read (an extra call; git prints the width); 64 hex for this repository's commits (objects of one known sha1 repository).
  - Fix: schemas — the IA schema and the request schema's draft (the draft's rule is held equal to the record's), staging, `approved-delta`, `execution-base`, `delivery-profile`, `evidence-manifest`, `project-status`, `iteration-result`, `static-analysis-result`, `verification-batch`, `feature-map`, `walkthrough` (`walkthrough_id` too), `run-record` (across its sprints) and each helper intent (one intent, one operation) carry the rule; `project-instructions-lock` ties its widths to `object_format`; `artifact-write-receipt` (one OID) is widened only. Left: the planning-publication operation records, the helper wire and the Tickets manifest (their validator holds the format: widths from `object_format`, derived records bound by equality, one width per wire exchange), `anchor-pack`, the issue inventory and the matrix (commits of this sha1 repository). Host — `readRef`, `reflogDepth`, `reflogEntries` and `listTree` read either format (R7-31); `acceptanceFacts` refuses a state whose base, staging commit and tree and named applied commits are of two formats, or a squash commit of another format than the base (`acceptance_missing`), so nothing is composed, presented, signed or accepted for it on either path (a base, staging commit or tree that is not a full OID is refused the same way, where before any non-empty string passed); `composeAuthorization` holds the applied commits to the base's format; `acceptanceErrors` refuses at the CAS a squash commit of another format than the base (`acceptance_missing`), the one acceptance OID compared with the record alone; `askCustody` reads a helper observation in the request's format (else `planning_ref_capability_missing`); `deliveryCompleted` reads the shared rule, behavior unchanged. No new refusal code; graph, matrix and vocabulary unchanged. A group's state is read from its matrix `injection`, whether the run detected its fault and whether a control was asked and stayed silent (`coverageState`, new `scripts/lib/clean-room-coverage.mjs`, shared by the run, `validate:clean-room` and the package): `covered_by_real_fault` (a `real_path` or `measured_observation` fault, detected, control silent), `covered_without_control` (the same, no control asked: F001–F003), `covered_by_written_observation` (a `written_observation` group whose guard answered), `control_failed` (detected, control not silent), `not_covered` (no harness ran it, or its fault went undetected); only `covered_by_real_fault` counts toward the gate, `complete` stays "every group `covered_by_real_fault`" and is false, and the exit status is unchanged (`ok` = every step ran). The addition: the table declares no detection — `harnessCoverage(steps)` reads F001–F004 from the `harness:crash` points and the `harness:identity` group and control, so a failed or absent harness covers nothing; otherwise F001–F004's states would still be declared. Rows carry `injection`, `detected` and a tri-state `control` (`null` = not asked), and `counts` all five states, zero included.   - Decision (the recommended one, with one change on evidence): 10g mirrored in the registry. Every class carries `lifecycle`; a class v1 does not govern names its owning issue in `decided_by`; the schema requires the field (a closed enum) and `decided_by` exactly when the class is not v1. The change: #47's two classes are `successor_matrix_candidate`, not `planned_after_v1` — `planned_after_v1` is the matrix's "inactive in v1 and mandatory for the full program", which no matrix says of #47, and 9d refuses exactly that claim in the contracts, so one vocabulary means the matrix's values where the matrix classifies the issue and the successor marker where it does not; the marker is what #47's contract and R6-28 ("a successor-matrix marker on those classes") say, and `static-analysis.md` now names it. So the enum has three values, not two. `validate:artifact-registry` holds `planned_after_v1` to a matrix `planned_after_v1` issue and `successor_matrix_candidate` to an issue the matrix does not classify, refuses a v1 class that names an issue or requires a class v1 does not govern, and holds every class the graph's predicates read to `required_for_v1` (every workflow the graph registers is v1's); a graph whose predicates read no class is refused. `classify()` picks the owner first, as for any path, and parks a path whose owner is not `required_for_v1` as `unknown_class`, naming the class (`candidates`), its `lifecycle` and `decided_by`, with no category, review, approval or publication; a class without the field is refused too. No new refusal code: `unknown_class` is what the classifier answers a path no class governs, and a class v1 does not govern is known to the registry and unknown to v1 — `carrier_mapping_unknown` of 10g, mirrored; the refusal vocabulary holds the graph's 98 park reasons, not the classifier's §8 set, so nothing moves there. Coverage counts v1 classes only: the repository-coverage check now parks a file governed only by a class v1 does not govern, and the validator prints the three counts apart. The impact closure is not filtered: `contract_document` impacts the non-v1 classes too, so a changeset touching their contracts lists them among the impacted classes (this commit's does); they have no instances in v1, so nothing is invalidated, and the impact graph stays the full program's.     - H1 (High): the ref-custody policy schema fixed the Git directories at `0700` under the custody owner account, which on Linux leaves the installing user no ordinary write (named POSIX ACL entries are masked by the group bits `0700` leaves empty), and the reason this entry, §5a, ADR-095's note, ADR-102 and the package gave for leaving it — changing the modes moves `policy_digest` — was wrong: the digest is computed over `ref-custody-policy.example.json`, not over the schema. Measured with Git 2.43 under three distinct uids (`12a/fix/perm-experiment.sh`, `perm-experiment-2.sh` and their logs in the session scratchpad), §5a now states the topology the Linux bootstrap writes: a group of the installing user and the custody owner account, never `autosk-model`; the service root root-owned with that group, `0750`, which the model account cannot enter; the common Git directory and its refs, logs, objects and `worktrees/` directories custody-owned with the group at `3770` (setgid, sticky) and `core.sharedRepository=0660`; `refs/autosk/`, `logs/refs/autosk/` and everything the helper writes under them (its Git with `core.sharedRepository=0640` and `core.hooksPath=/dev/null`), `packed-refs`, `config`, the common `HEAD`, `hooks/`, `info/` and `objects/info/` custody-owned and closed to the group. The first topology tried was wrong twice, and the measurement showed it: `hooks/` was group-writable, and the directories the helper created under `refs/autosk/` were too, so the installing user deleted a protected ref. The schema admits `3770` beside `0700` for both profile modes and the topology entry; `validate:platform-support` holds that it does and that no admitted mode lets another account in (`policyModeErrors`); the example and its digest `a9802252…` are unchanged, and its profiles change only when #5 signs the goldens that carry it again, since the daemon key the example binds has no private half in the repository. The model account's worktree writes succeeded only with a group-writable checkout (umask `002`; under `022` they were denied), which is why the worktree's modes stay open with its placement. L5: the round's test that pinned `0700` is inverted.
    - M1: `security.ref_custody` was listed by the `planning` and `delivery` phases, so only `autosk-planned` required it, while Quick (from `intake`, one edge to `init_planning_ref`) and a Ticket (from `implement`, through `freeze` to `narrow_review_join`) reach steps that ask the helper. It is derived from the graph like the model-step checks: every workflow whose first step reaches a step the `planning_ref_capability_missing` row parks at requires it (`CUSTODY_STEP_CHECKS`, `custodySteps`, `asksCustody` in `src/host/workflow-preflight.mjs`) — `autosk-planned`, `autosk-quick` and `autosk-ticket` — and a graph without that row, or with an empty `parks_at`, is refused (`doctor_required_set_unsatisfied`). A test holds that every action a host driver hands `askCustody` runs at a step of that set, and `installCheckErrors` checks the custody record's `checked_by` against `CUSTODY_STEP_CHECKS`. The doctor contract, README, 02, ADR-095's note, ADR-102, #34's obligation and the package say so.
    - M2: §5b, 02 §6, README rule 16, #11's obligation and ADR-102 (its first alternative included) say where a model's provider session, `HOME` and configuration live: a session directory of its own outside `.autosk`, whose store accepts only files private to autoskd's uid; autoskd reads a session file from it as untrusted input, parsed and copied, never adopted into its store. Where that directory lies under the project root is open for #13 with #18 and #11.
    - M3: autoskd cannot signal a process of another uid (`kill(2)` needs the same uid or `CAP_KILL`), so the launch mechanism gives it whole-tree termination: a service-manager unit whose control group the manager kills satisfies it; a sudoers rule alone does not, since sudo relays a signal only to its child, and with one the rule starts a supervisor under the model account that kills each tree whole. The schema and record hold `whole_tree_termination=true`; #13's obligation and verification and #18's obligation carry it; §8 asks for a forking, double-forking, session-leaving tree killed whole.
    - M4: autoskd never discovers a repository from a directory the model account can write: every Git command for a model worktree names `--git-dir` and `--work-tree`, turns hooks, `core.fsmonitor` and submodule recursion off and inherits no Git variable, and the install's `safe.directory` exceptions name only the worktrees whose Git directories are custody-owned, never `*` (measured with Git 2.43: named explicitly, Git read the true state and ran neither the planted repository's fsmonitor nor its hook; discovering, it refused the model-owned gitfile as dubious ownership, which an explicit `--git-dir` skips). Measured over the drivers: only the aggregate driver discovered from a worktree — `checkoutStaging` read `rev-parse HEAD` with `cwd` in the checkout, where the checks run model-written code. It now reads the checkout from `git worktree list --porcelain` (the path and `HEAD` of each worktree from the common Git directory, no worktree's `.git` opened): exactly one new worktree, at the staging commit; the tree comes from the commit, and no Git command runs from inside the checkout. The other drivers call their injected runner without `cwd`; the rule for that runner is #18's obligation, and aggregate checks run under the model account (#9's and #18's obligations). §5b and §8 say so, and #13's obligation names the `safe.directory` exceptions.
    - L1: Git 2.43 replaces `packed-refs` on every ref deletion, even of a loose ref with `packed-refs` empty, header-only or absent, so the installing user cannot delete an ordinary branch or tag while `packed-refs` is custody's, and unpacking every ref at install does not help; which account deletes an ordinary ref is open for #5 and #13, and §8 asks for the case. L2: the model account cannot read autoskd's RPC token (`~/.autosk/daemon-token`, `0600`) and finds its TCP listener only on loopback (upstream's default without `--tcp` is `0.0.0.0:7077`); #13's probe proves both. L3: §5a maps the policy's `project-account`, the "project account" of `epic-planning-ref.md` and `epic-staging.md`, and the policy's `helper_account` and `owner_account` onto the three accounts. L4: §5b names what one shared uid does not isolate — files readable to all, `/proc` command lines without `hidepid`, a shared `/tmp`, and model processes signalling or ptracing each other.

The coordinator decided a redesign (fix round 2, a new commit on `0ea81de`): no custody service account; the project's Git directory stays the installing user's ordinary repository; the helper runs as the installing user, a separate process with its own socket, intents, journal and nonces, and stays the only writer of `refs/autosk/**` by protocol; the model account is the OS boundary; the privileged install creates only `autosk-model`, its group and the launch mechanism. Measured before it was written (Git 2.43, two numeric uids; `12a/fix2/exp-redesign.sh`, `exp-bare.sh`, `exp-sudo.py` and their logs in the session scratchpad): the user's porcelain works on the repository (`branch -D`, `tag -d`, `git config`, `gc`); with `.git` at `0700` the model account cannot open it and its own `git` in its worktree finds no repository; in a worktree autoskd creates under umask `007` (root and directories `2770` setgid for the worktree group, files `0660`, gitfile pinned `0644`) the model edits and creates files, cannot rewrite the gitfile in place, and can replace it — then the user's Git refuses the worktree as dubious ownership and autoskd's explicit `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` reads the true state; with the project's `core.hooksPath=.husky`, the user's `git commit` in the model worktree ran the model's hook as the user unless the worktree's `config.worktree` pins `core.hooksPath=/dev/null`; an implicit bare repository the model builds inside a directory the user checked out passes Git's ownership check and ran the model's `diff.external` as the user, and `safe.bareRepository=explicit` refuses it; a protected ref the user's tool moves is refused by the helper's next expected-old CAS; `git pack-refs --all` packs a protected ref despite `gc.packRefs=false`; `git replace` changes what `rev-parse <commit>^{tree}` answers, and `--no-replace-objects` reads the true tree; under `Defaults use_pty` (sudo 1.9.15p5), a SIGTERM to sudo ended the command it started while a background child in its process group and a `setsid` descendant kept running. Each finding, and what the redesign did with it:
    - NH1 (the installing user plants `commondir` in the common directory's root, and both its Git and the helper's read a forged planning ref; the helper follows the user's `refs/replace/*`): falls away with mixed ownership — the Git directory is the principal's own, and against the principal's tools the helper relies on detection, not on the OS. The helper's and autoskd's Git reads run with `--no-replace-objects`, so a replace ref changes nothing they read (measured; §5a, `epic-planning-ref.md`).
    - NH2 (the `safe.directory` entries §5a and #13 prescribed hand a model the user's uid): falls away — no exception is needed, since a model worktree's root, gitfile and administrative directory are the user's; README advises never an exception for a model worktree, `*` least of all, and `safe.bareRepository=explicit`. The claims that the model cannot write its worktree's gitfile or that the gitfile is read-only (§5b, §8, `epic-planning-ref.md`, 02 §2, 03 §9) are corrected: the model can replace it, which the user's Git then refuses and which neither autoskd nor the helper reads. Every model worktree's `config.worktree` pins `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and `core.fsmonitor=false` (bootstrap: `extensions.worktreeConfig`; #18 writes it).
    - NH3 (custody maintenance at `0640` breaks the user's writes, at `0660` opens the protected reflogs to the user): falls away — no custody account writes the repository.
    - H1 (partly closed): 0ea81de's `3770` admission and `policyModeErrors` are undone; the policy schema admits the ADR-102 profile (`helper_runs_as=installing-user`: helper and owner accounts the installing user, `gitfile_read_only=false`, one topology entry, the common Git directory at `0700`, `project-account` the model account) beside the unchanged example (digest `a9802252…`), and `validate:platform-support` holds the example, its ADR-102 form and the refusal of a mix (`adr102PolicyProfile`, `policyProfileErrors`).
    - Medium 4–6 (L1's premise false — plumbing `update-ref -d` passes, porcelain `branch -D`, `tag -d`, `fetch --prune` fail; README 19 silent that config, hooks and info were closed to the user; ownership after migration and inside per-worktree directories unstated): fall away with the user-owned repository — the user's porcelain works (measured), nothing of its repository is closed to it, and nothing migrates. §5a and §8 say so, and README 19 is rewritten.
    - Low 7 (topology enum not tied to `path_role`), 8 (`policyModeErrors` checked only the other bits) and 10 (`helper_account` vs `owner_account`): fall away with the undo — the ADR-102 branch ties its one topology entry to `project-common-git-dir` at `0700`, and both accounts are the installing user; §5a maps the older names once (`project-account` is the model account; the example's `helper_account` and `owner_account` are the withdrawn service accounts). - 2026-09-28: debt 12b (R8-2, R8-3, R8-15, R8-16): the acceptance path is live and bound — an acceptance does not move what it accepts (an Epic's own acceptance decision and the IntegrationAuthorizationRecord it completes stay out of the heads the record names), v1 has one acceptance authority, the person's signature at `accept_staging` (a pinned auto-policy is held to the same binding, gains no autonomy under it and is reached by no v1 edge; an unattended acceptance needs a different binding, #28's own post-v1 design — as corrected on review, M1), a resume decision binds the project, and the authorization chain is per scope under one head kept for integrity. R8-2: `HEAD_FACTS` (`src/host/staging-acceptance.mjs:44-49`), `acceptanceFacts` (`:101-104`), `composeAuthorization` (`:196-198`) and `assertComposedFor` (`:229-231`) bind the three heads as the caller hands them before the question; `acceptanceFromDecision` (`:341-347`) rebuilds the facts from the heads in force at the answer — `accepted` with the heads as asked, `acceptance_stale` ("The staging identity moved between the question and the record") with heads the Epic's own decision moved, which is what IA §3, 01 §2 (`:128`, the daemon commits the authority head before it returns the record), 01 §5 (`:249`) and 03 (`:364`, `:1007`: only unrelated appends are exempt) read literally give. No host module computes a head: the three occur under `src/` only in `staging-acceptance.mjs`, taken from `heads.*` or `facts.*`. R8-15: `resumeDecisionSubject` (`src/host/user-decision.mjs:156-158`) digests exactly `task_id, reason, watermark, target`, and `resumeDecisionAdmitter` (`:179-193`) compares no project — a record of project `bbbb…` for task `t-1` was admitted (`true`) by an admitter handed project `0000…`.   - Decision (the recommended one on all four, kept on evidence): R8-2 — an acceptance does not move what it accepts: the scope's own acceptance (the `UserDecisionRecord` answering its acceptance question, subject the staging identity, and the record an `accept` completes) never enters its relevant authority projection, dependency head or intent head; storing them moves the global authority head (integrity only) and `integration_authorization_head`, never a head the record names; `integrateApproved` compares under that exclusion, and anything else relevant after the question still stales the acceptance. Stated once in IA §3, pointed to from 01 §2/§5, 02 §7 (the `authorityGuard` paragraph) and §5 (the record's), 03 §2 (`integrate_staging`) and §4 (`authorityGuard`), `cond_441`. No host code computes heads, so it is an obligation of #4 (the heads) and #9 (`integrateApproved`'s comparison) inside matrix v1 (ADR-097's rule), not #18 (its guard-authority evaluator decides who takes `t_556`, not what the heads hold), held by prose tests and named in the package's not-claimed list. R8-3 — the graph is right: v1 has one acceptance authority, the user's signature at the stop over the exact post-aggregate identity; the documents (IA §1 and status, `epic-staging.md` §5, 01 §7 preamble and step 5, 01 §8 and 03 §7 view rows, `cond_435`, `cond_436`, `cond_438`, 02 §5, 03 §2 and §5, the module's comments) no longer offer a pinned auto-policy that skips the stop; `autoPolicyAcceptance` stays exported and checked, its comment and the contract say no v1 edge reaches it, and #9's obligation says v1 has one authority; the first fix called it the binding #28's unattended path must pass, which the review found no unattended path can pass (M1, below): under it a policy adds no autonomy, #9's criterion reads under v1 as every acceptance, a policy's included, carrying the user's signature over the exact identity, and an unattended acceptance needs a different binding, #28's own post-v1 design work, reviewed by a successor panel; `validate:integration-authorization` holds both (`acceptanceAuthorityErrors`, and since the review `acceptanceAuthority`). R8-15 — `resumeDecisionSubject({ project_root_sha256, task_id, reason, watermark, target })`; `resumeDecisionAdmitter({ projectRootSha256, record, verifySignature })` is one project's: without a 64-hex project it admits nothing and reads no record, and a record whose `project_root_sha256` or subject is another project's is `resume_target_not_permitted`; the factory is unchanged and still imports only the canonical form. R8-16 — one `integration_authorization_head` per project for integrity (anti-rollback), a chain per scope (`previous_authorization_head_hash` = the previous record of the same `scope_id`), `integrateApproved` compares the scope's head, so another Epic's or Quick run's record does not stale this one; anti-rollback holds because the global head commits to every scope's chain. Forms not taken: heads as of the decision's `previous_secure_head_hash` (freezes the check at the answer, so a relevant revoke or supersede before the CAS would no longer stale it, and needs per-Epic heads by past global head); a policy guard on `t_556`/`t_557` plus a pre-acceptance signing step (the same person's signature on the same identity, so a path without autonomy); removing `autoPolicyAcceptance` and `pinned_auto_policy` (nothing in v1 reaches them, and removal changes the CAS admission and schema and #28 would re-add it); a `resume-decision/v2` domain (no record was ever signed under the old preimage); the factory naming the project (it does not know it); a per-project chain (a liveness coupling between Epics the one CAS does not need).

  - Measured on `c3a6d75`, by running code (probes `12d/measure/probe-base.mjs`, `probe-parks.mjs` and their logs in the session scratchpad). `classify()` (`src/host/artifact-classifier.mjs:85-137`) parked a path no class governs as `unknown_class` (`:91`), a path classes of two categories claim as `ambiguous_class` (`:98-103`), and a path whose owner is not `required_for_v1` as `unknown_class` too, naming the class, its lifecycle and `decided_by` (`:118-126`); its `PARK_REASONS` (`:16-25`) was the contract's §8 set of eight (`docs/contracts/artifact-registry.md:87`), `unknown_class`, `ambiguous_class` and `unregistered_artifact` among them. None of those names is a recovery row of the graph (98 rows) or a vocabulary entry (98), so `permitsResume` refused every target of such a park (`resume_target_not_permitted: unknown_class has no recovery row`): the task could only be cancelled. 01 §2 (`01-core-flows.md:144`) called the registry's refusal of an ungoverned path `unregistered_artifact`, "the same refusal named otherwise" as `artifact_mapping_required`; the vocabulary (`resources/refusal-vocabulary/refusal-vocabulary.v1.json:514`) recorded `artifact_mapping_required` as `daemon`, with no files, closed by 03; the registry validator named an unlisted contract `unregistered_artifact` (`scripts/validate-artifact-registry.mjs:371`); `parkKind` (`scripts/classify-changeset.mjs:54-59`) told the kinds apart by the result's shape (a `lifecycle` field or none). The graph's row (`resources/workflow-graph/workflow-graph.v1.json:14021`) parks at `freeze` (`t_101`, `guard_101`, priority 0) and `freeze_artifact` (`t_100`, `guard_100`, priority 13) on `cond_024`, is handled at `clarify_alignment`, `invalidate_quick_classification` and `present_tickets_breakdown`, and resumes into `await_alignment`, `draft_artifact`, `human`, `record_alignment`, `validate_tickets_manifest`. The classifier's verdict routes a candidate at those two steps only: `freeze`, a Quick's or a Ticket's code (edges into `dispatch_review`, `dispatch_narrow_review`, `record_editorial_exemption`, `record_code_verdict`; its only way in besides its self-loop is `t_418` from `verify`; 03 §2's row reads "freeze_artifact / Quick freeze / Ticket freeze", `03-technical-plan.md:115`), and `freeze_artifact`, a planning artifact (into `dispatch_panel`, `dispatch_narrow_review`, `record_artifact_pass`); Arena's `freeze_candidate` routes nothing by class, `commit_on_pass` compares the re-minted tree with the approved one (`candidate_changed`), and the other callers, `editorialExemption` and `bootstrapDecision`, read `status` alone. Resume under the row (`permitsResume`): from `freeze_artifact`, `draft_artifact` and `human`, and `await_alignment` and `record_alignment` (and `validate_tickets_manifest`) once `clarify_alignment` (or `present_tickets_breakdown`) completed its handling under the park; from `freeze`, only `human` and the Epic's `draft_artifact` (04 risk 6). The other five §8 names park nothing: `impactClosure` throws `registry_drift` (`:155`), the validator refuses a cycle by message, and `missing_predecessor`, `validator_missing` and `schema_missing` are named only by the two lists and a comment. `produce:refusals` does not measure `artifact_mapping_required` (64 classes), and none of the four contracts it has a manifest for declares it.
  - Decision (the recommended one, with two adjustments on evidence): one stop, one name, one producer, one recovery row. Every park of the classifier is `artifact_mapping_required` (`PARK_REASON`), and the park carries a closed `cause` (`PARK_CAUSES`): `unknown_class` (no class governs the path; `candidates` empty), `ambiguous_class` (classes of two categories; `candidates` those classes), `class_not_v1` (its owner is a class v1 does not govern; `candidates`, `lifecycle`, `decided_by` as since ADR-101). The not-v1 case gets its own cause: `parkKind` already told it apart, by the result's shape, and its remedy differs (the owning issue, not a registry entry), so `unknown_class` no longer names two things — the review of 11g's L2, which ADR-101 left open with the `unregistered_artifact`/`unknown_class` split. `classifyChangeset` carries the same park per path; `impactClosure` still throws `unknown_class` for a class name the registry lacks — the same fact, thrown rather than parked. The vocabulary entry is `host`, `src/host/artifact-classifier.mjs`, closed by `docs/contracts/artifact-registry.md`: the producer rule refutes `daemon` once runtime code names the code, and the owner rule refutes `03-technical-plan.md` once the contract's closed set does; `named_at` is unchanged (03 §7's row names the same five steps); resealed. The contract's §8 closes `artifact_mapping_required`, `missing_predecessor`, `registry_drift`, `cyclic_impact_graph`, `validator_missing`, `schema_missing`, says which stop and row a parked path takes and where it resumes, names the three causes (`unregistered_artifact` as `unknown_class`'s older name) and says the other five park nothing here; §4 says the park is one stop told apart by its cause; 01 §2 names the stop, its causes and its producer. `validate:artifact-registry` requires the contract to name each cause and holds the graph to a recovery row for the classifier's reason that parks at `freeze` and `freeze_artifact` (`CLASSIFIED_AT`, `parkRowErrors`); it names an unlisted contract `(unknown_class)`. The CLI prints `PARKED artifact_mapping_required (<cause>) …` and picks the remedy by the cause. Adjusted on evidence: (1) no graph change — the step the brief named as a possible gap, a Ticket's code freeze, is `freeze`, where the row already parks, and no other step needs the verdict, so the graph's `canonical_digest` does not move; (2) no `produce:refusals` case — a case in any of the four manifests is `undeclared` (their contracts do not declare the name), and a manifest for the registry contract would have to cover every §8 name, three of which nothing produces; the 03 §7 manifest is debt 9g (04 risk 10), as ADR-086 said of `review_cap`. The host entry is held by the textual check, as every unmeasured host entry is (`emission_checked` stays 7 of 98), and the behavior by the runtime test. Forms not taken: a recovery row of `unknown_class`'s own (its recovery is `artifact_mapping_required`'s — two rows for one stop is the defect); `unknown_class` kept for the not-v1 case (ADR-101's `carrier_mapping_unknown` mirror; L2 stays open); `unregistered_artifact` kept as a refusal of its own (its only producer was the validator's message for a path the classifier parks as `unknown_class`); `origin_edges` with each workflow's targets on the row (risk 6's closure): the scoped row's surface is its origin, so the Epic's stop at `freeze_artifact` would lose `await_alignment`, `record_alignment` and `validate_tickets_manifest`, which handled-at completion receipts lend and which are no edge out of `freeze_artifact` — a change to the Epic's recovery with no finding behind it.

R8-7: F002 (`resources/clean-room-e2e/fault-matrix.v1.json:50-55`, "kill the daemon, restart, repeat the same creation key") and F003 (`:77-82`, "SIGKILL between the transcript header and the meta write") say in their own `injection_note` that the run does neither — the crash harness kills the native store writer the daemon spawns, at the task-record write and at the second creation-index write; the daemon is not killed or restarted and no transcript or meta is written. `coverageState` (`scripts/lib/clean-room-coverage.mjs:54`) read the group's `injection`, the run's detection and its control and never whether the run was the designed fault; the fields of a group it could read were `id, boundary, description, fault, injection, injection_note, proofs, expected_gate_outcome`, so the departure lived in prose only. Measured: F002 and F003 reach `covered_without_control` today and `covered_by_real_fault` once #36 adds a control. Ten more groups' notes admit the same departure — F006, F007, F008, F012, F015–F020 ("no restore runs", "no CAS runs", "no recovery runs", "no injector runs", "no process is killed", "not a second process"); F001, F004, F005, F009, F010, F011, F013 and F014 name none. R8-12: the run's gate count was 6 — F004 and the five `measured_observation` groups (F005, F009, F010, F013, F014: `locationErrors`, `collisionErrors`, `environmentErrors`, `batchSufficiencyErrors`, `waitExceeded`) — although no case of the fault harness runs a host driver, the daemon or a helper (`clean-room-e2e.md:81`). The weighed exception (a measured guard that is the only implementation of the boundary and that the product itself calls on that path): the guards' drivers — `snapshot-driver`, `delta-driver` (under `execution-base-driver`), `work-type-dispatcher`, `preflight-runner` — are imported by no module outside `test/`, and there is no product entry point (the extension's is #18's) — so none of the five meets the fault on a product path.
  - Decision (the recommended one, kept; the field's form adjusted on evidence): R8-12 — only `real_path` counts; `measured_observation` gets `covered_by_host_function`, reported as what it is, not counted; none of the five is counted, by the measurement above. R8-7 — an explicit boolean of every group, `injection_matches_design`, plus `design_departure` on a group that departs (and none on one that does not), read by `coverageState`: a `real_path` group whose flag is not `true` is `covered_by_substitute_fault` (`control_failed` ranks first: a control that did not stay silent makes the detection say nothing). Twelve groups depart (F002, F003, F006–F008, F012, F015–F020), eight match. Seven states, in the rule's order: `covered_by_real_fault`, `covered_without_control`, `covered_by_substitute_fault`, `covered_by_host_function`, `covered_by_written_observation`, `control_failed`, `not_covered`. `product_path_owner` on each of the 18 groups that are not the designed fault on the product path (all but F001 and F004; F001 does not count either, and awaits a control) starts with #36 and names what must exist first — nothing for F002, F003 and F005 (the run builds the daemon whose writes answer them); #8's delta driver for F009, F010 and F007; #9's staging and final-CAS driver for F006, F007, F015, F016 (F008: #8's, its guard reached through `integrationProof`); #24's batch runner for F012 and F013; #26's preflight runner for F011 and F014; #5's ref-custody helper under #13's account model for F017–F020; each reached from the extension entry point (#18). The validator holds the fields (the flag required; `design_departure` iff false; `product_path_owner` iff the group is not the designed fault on the product path and starting with #36; a tripwire on a closed list of phrases that refuses a note admitting a departure beside a flag saying none — a tripwire, not a parser, which is why the rule reads the field). The gate's count is 1 (F004): with every fault-harness case detected and silent, `covered_by_real_fault` 1, `covered_without_control` 1 (F001), `covered_by_substitute_fault` 2, `covered_by_host_function` 5, `covered_by_written_observation` 11; `complete=false`. Forms not taken: the departure as an `injection` kind (`injection` says where the fault lands and is held to the harness's `CASES`; F006 would be two kinds at once); parsing `injection_note` (free text is not reliably parsed); counting the measured groups with a note on the row (R8-12 itself); counting a measured group whose guard is the boundary's only implementation (nothing on a product path calls any of the five); rewriting F002's and F003's `description`/`fault` to the run (ADR-100's first alternative); making the run do the designed faults here (#36's harness).

The facts are not a partition of meaning: `task_record` is read by 175 predicates, in 23 domains with 15 owners once the domains below are drawn, `phase` by 83 (7 domains, 6 owners), `kind` 31 (7, 6), `status` 22 (9, 8), `candidate_keepalive` 31, `pending_anchor` 23; 109 of the 141 facts are read inside one domain and one owner, 32 across several, and `classifier_verdict` by one predicate (`cond_024`). #18's obligation named three example domains in prose (`resources/program-capabilities/matrix.v1.json`, the sentence "Each predicate's meaning stays with its domain record's module (…)") and the only check was the test matching those three sentences (`test/validate-program-capability-matrix.test.mjs`, the "#18 owns the evaluator mechanism …" test), so a graph predicate with no owner passed. The daemon sees only the graph's digest (patches `0032`, `0045`), so a field on a predicate needs no autosk patch; the factory reads predicate ids only.
  - Decision (the recommended one, in its second form, on evidence): every predicate's meaning has a named `required_for_v1` owner, derived and checked. Not a fact→owner table: the measurement above (`task_record` alone is 175 of 433 reads and means something else in each) would either collapse the generic facts onto #18, whose obligation is the mechanism, or name every record as their owner. Not `owner: N` on each predicate either: it ties the generic graph document to program issue numbers (an owner change would move the graph's digest), and no obligation can name 433 ids for the validator to hold by text. Taken: a closed, optional `domain` (id) on the predicate, in the digest, and a closed matrix field `predicate_domains` (`domain`, `decision`, `owner_issues` — exactly one, `elements`, `delivery`) beside `enforcement_points`, held by `validateOwnership` and `validateReverseClaims` as the primitives and the points are (the obligation names `` `<domain>` `` and the surface "meaning of the `<domain>` predicates"); `predicateDomainRequirements(graph)` reads the domains and the unowned predicates from the graph. The graph registers only v1 workflows, so a post-v1 owner is refused; no rule for a post-v1 workflow's predicates is invented. The 433 predicates were assigned once, by the rule ADR-107 states (the record whose module decides the guard's condition; where a predicate joins two records' clauses, the one whose clause decides the transition), and are held from now on; the assignment is a design judgment for the panel to read. 26 domains, 17 owners, all `required_for_v1`; no matrix edge added (five owners — #9, #15, #16, #32, #35 — depend on #18, so an edge #18 → owner would be a cycle the matrix refuses for them, and adding it for the others alone would draw one relation two ways; the table and the owners' modules meet at integration, #36).
